--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
This is an AGGREGATION mission - robots need to gather in the BLUE-bordered strip (black floor). Score increases when robots are outside the target region. So robots need to:
1. Navigate to the black-floor strip (the target)
2. Stay there

**Key observations from Round 1:**
- Best score: 19898 with simple single-state Exploration (B=0, rwm=37)
- This is surprisingly good - random walk apparently gets robots into the strip and keeps them there somewhat
- The mission description says target is the BLUE-bordered strip with BLACK floor
- Wait, re-reading: "score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region" - this is AGGREGATION to a specific location, not inter-robot aggregation!

**Key insight:** The target is the BLACK-floor strip (BLUE walls). Robots should:
1. Move toward the black floor (GoToColor with blue color?)
2. Stay on black floor once there

Wait - GoToColor moves toward colored objects. The blue walls border the target strip. So `B=8, clr=3` (blue=3? Let me check colors: {1,2,3,4,5,6}). Colors for walls: blue walls border target strip.

Actually looking at rank 13: `--s0 9 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 6 --clr0 3` (GoAwayColor, color 3) scored 23997 - worst. And rank 2 used `--clr1 4` (GoToColor yellow?).

Floor conditions: C=0=BLACK, C=1=GRAY, C=2=WHITE. Target has BLACK floor. So when on GRAY, move toward blue walls. When on BLACK, stop or stay.

**Strategy:**
- State 0: Exploration/movement to find black floor
- When floor is BLACK (C=0): stay/stop or transition to attraction
- When floor is GRAY (C=1): keep moving toward blue walls

GoToColor with blue (color likely = 3 based on cle colors: 0=off,4=yellow,5=magenta,6=cyan... but clr uses {1,2,3,4,5,6} which might be red=1,green=2,blue=3,yellow=4,magenta=5,cyan=6).

Key design: Use floor color detection. On BLACK floor = in target zone, stay. On GRAY = not in target, move.

**My machines:**

1. **Champion (elitism):** Copy exactly
2. **Exploitation variants of champion:** Adjust rwm
3. **2-state: explore→stop on black floor**
4. **2-state: GoToBlue walls → stop on black**
5. **Various combinations**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key observations from Round 2:**

Massive improvement! Score dropped from ~19898 to 6507. The champion is:
- State 0: Exploration (rwm=37) → go to State 1 if BLACK floor (p=0.9) OR random (p=0.1)
- State 1: Stop → go to State 0 if GRAY floor (p=0.85) OR random (p=0.03)  
- State 2: GoToColor blue (clr=3) → go to State 1 if BLACK floor (p=0.9)

**Critical insight:** The pattern that works:
1. Explore until on black floor → Stop (stay in target)
2. If accidentally on gray, go back to exploring or GoToColor(blue)
3. GoToColor(blue) is the key addition - it actively navigates toward blue walls = target strip

**What's working:**
- GoToColor(clr=3=blue) + floor detection is the winning combo
- Stop on black floor is essential
- The champion has a "shortcut" transition from explore → GoToColor randomly (but actually goes to Stop/State1)

Wait, let me re-read: State 0 transitions: n0x0→State1 on BLACK (p=0.9), n0x1→State1 on random (p=0.1). So State 2 (GoToColor blue) is only reachable from... nowhere? State 2 only goes to State 1. State 0 and State 1 both go to State 1 or State 0. State 2 is unreachable!

Actually wait: with --n0x0 1 pointing to destination 1 (among non-self states [1,2], so D=1→State2), --n0x1 1 pointing to destination 1→State2... No wait:

With 3 states, leaving state 0, others are [1,2]. D=0→State1, D=1→State2. So n0x0 D=1→State1, n0x1 D=1→State1. Both go to State1. State2 is indeed unreachable from State0!

But State1 transitions: n1x0→D=0→State0 (gray), n1x1→D=0→State0 (random). State2 is unreachable entirely!

So the champion is effectively 2-state: Explore→Stop, with GoToColor as dead weight. The key is the floor-detection: stop when black, explore when gray.

The 2nd place (7867) is GoToColor(blue)→Stop on black, which IS using GoToColor actively.

**New strategy:** Make GoToColor reachable AND combine with floor detection:
- State 0: Explore → State 2 (GoToColor blue) randomly, State 1 (Stop) on black
- State 1: Stop → State 0 (explore) on gray
- State 2: GoToColor(blue) → State 1 (Stop) on black

Let me design variants:

1. Champion (exact copy)
2. Make GoToColor reachable from explore: Explore→GoToColor(random), GoToColor→Stop(black), Stop→Explore(gray)
3. Tune probabilities of champion topology
4. Add neighbor condition to stop state
5. Try GoToColor as primary state with floor transitions
6. 4-state: explore, GoToColor, stop, re-explore

- State 0: GoToColor(clr=3), n0x0: D=1→State1 on BLACK(p=0.9), n0x1: D=1→State1 on GRAY(p=0.3)
- State 1: Stop, n1x0: D=1→State2 on GRAY(p=0.85), n1x1: D=0→State0 on random(p=0.03)
- State 2: Explore(rwm=37), n2x0: D=1→State1 on BLACK(p=0.9), n2x1: D=0→State0 on GRAY(p=0.7)

So the flow is:
- GoToColor(blue) → Stop when on black OR gray (p=0.3)
- Stop → Explore when on gray (p=0.85), → GoToColor randomly (p=0.03)
- Explore → Stop when on black (p=0.9), → GoToColor when on gray (p=0.7)

This makes sense! GoToColor(blue) gets robots to blue wall = target strip. Floor detection confirms arrival. Stop keeps them there. Explore and GoToColor alternate when gray (not in target).

**Key patterns that work well (top 5, all ~6400-7800):**
1. GoToColor(blue) as primary + Stop on black + Explore as fallback
2. Floor detection (BLACK=stop, GRAY=move) is crucial
3. Transition from GRAY floor triggers movement behaviors
4. The gray-floor transition from Stop→somewhere is important (rank 1 has p=0.85 for this)

**What to try:**
- Fine-tune probabilities of the champion
- Try GoToColor with different approach: also add neighbor conditions
- Try 4-state with more sophisticated logic
- Try adjusting the GRAY transition probability from Stop (currently 0.85 - maybe lower?)
- Try adding a camera-based condition (see blue walls) to trigger transitions

**Key insight from rank 2 (6909):** Explore→Stop on black(p=0.95), Explore→GoToColor on gray(p=0.8). This is slightly different routing.

Let me tune the champion's key parameters:
- p0x1 (GoToColor→Stop on gray): 0.3 → try 0.2, 0.4, 0.5
- p1x0 (Stop→Explore on gray): 0.85 → try 0.7, 0.9
- p2x1 (Explore→GoToColor on gray): 0.7 → try 0.5, 0.8, 0.9

Also try: GoToColor as starting state with camera conditions for blue walls.

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 4:**

Excellent progress! Score improved from 6394 to 5947. The champion is a 4-state machine that is essentially the 3-state champion with an extra GoToColor(blue) state reachable from Stop.

Let me parse the champion carefully:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue/clr=3) → State1(Stop) if BLACK(p=0.9), → State1(Stop) if GRAY(p=0.3)
- State 1: Stop → State2(Explore) if GRAY(p=0.85), → State0(GoToColor) if random(p=0.03)
- State 2: Explore(rwm=37) → State1(Stop) if BLACK(p=0.9), → State0(GoToColor) if GRAY(p=0.7)
- State 3: GoToColor(blue/clr=3) → State1(Stop) if BLACK(p=0.9)

State 3 is unreachable from any state! So effectively the champion is the 3-state machine (states 0,1,2). But wait - state 1 goes to state 0 (D=0 from state 1 in 4-state = state 0) with p=0.03, not state 3. So state 3 is indeed unreachable.

The key structure that works:
- GoToColor(blue) as entry point
- Stop on black floor
- Explore when gray, then GoToColor when gray from explore
- The GoToColor→Stop transition on GRAY (p=0.3) is interesting

**Key parameters to tune:**
- p1x0 (Stop→Explore on gray): tried 0.7, 0.85, 0.9 - all competitive
- p2x1 (Explore→GoToColor on gray): 0.7-0.9 both good, 0.5 worse
- p0x1 (GoToColor→Stop on gray): 0.2-0.4 range, 0.3 seems best

**New explorations:**
1. Make state 3 reachable (4-state with proper connections)
2. Try different explore behavior (spiral, zigzag) instead of rwm
3. Try higher/lower probabilities
4. Add camera-based (blue color perception) transitions
5. Try GoAwayColor(green=2) instead of GoToColor(blue=3) - robots go away from green walls = toward blue walls

**Key insight:** GoAwayColor(green) should be equivalent to GoToColor(blue) since they're on opposite ends. Let me try clr=2 (green) with GoAwayColor(B=9).

Also: the 4-state champion has state 3 unreachable - what if I make a proper 4-state with state 3 reachable from stop or explore?

Wait, with 4 states, leaving state 1, the other states are [0,2,3]. D=0→State0, D=1→State3(?), D=2→State2. Let me re-read:
- n1x0: D=2→ (with 4 states, from state 1, others indexed as: 0=State0, 1=State2, 2=State3) → D=2→State3? 

Actually the indexing: from state 1, the other states are {0,2,3}, indexed D=0→State0, D=1→State2, D=2→State3.

So n1x0: D=2→State3, n1x1: D=0→State0, n1x2: D=2→State3 again? That would make State2 unreachable from State1!

Wait, let me re-read: --n1x0 2 means D=2 = the 3rd other state from state 1. From state 1, others are [0,2,3] → D=0→0, D=1→2, D=2→3. So n1x0 D=2 → State3! And n1x2 D=2 → State3 again.

So State2(Explore) is unreachable from State1! State3(GoToColor) gets transitions from State1.

So the actual flow:
- State0(GoToColor) → State1(Stop) always (on black or gray)
- State1(Stop) → State3(GoToColor) on GRAY(p=0.85) or random(p=0.05), → State0(GoToColor) random(p=0.03)
- State2(Explore) → State1(Stop) on BLACK, → State0(GoToColor) on GRAY - but **State2 is unreachable!**
- State3(GoToColor) → State1(Stop) on BLACK

So effectively: GoToColor→Stop→GoToColor cycle, with Stop→GoToColor(alt) backup. State2 and the explore behavior is dead weight!

The champion is essentially: GoToColor(blue) until BLACK floor → Stop → GoToColor(blue) when GRAY floor.

**Key insight:** The winning strategy is just GoToColor(blue) ↔ Stop, controlled by floor color. No exploration needed!

**New designs focusing on this insight:**
1. Pure 2-state: GoToColor(blue) ↔ Stop (floor-controlled)
2. Tune the champion's key probabilities
3. Add a 3rd useful state that's actually reachable

The critical parameters:
- p0x0: GoToColor→Stop on BLACK (0.9 seems good)
- p0x1: GoToColor→Stop on GRAY (0.3 - prevents overshooting?)
- p1x0: Stop→GoToColor on GRAY (0.85)
- p3x0: GoToColor(state3)→Stop on BLACK (0.9)

Let me design around this pure GoToColor↔Stop paradigm:

FSM: --nstates 4 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 5 --clr0 3 --n0 2 --n0x0 1 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.9 --n0x1 1 --c0x1 1 --p0x1 0.3 --s1 1 --cle1 5 --n1 3 --n1x0 2 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.85 --n1x1 0 --c1x1 5 --p1x1 0.03 --n1x2 2 --c1x2 5 --p1x2 0.05 --s2 0 --rwm2 37 --cle2 5 --n2 2 --n2x0 1 --c2x0 0 --p2x0 0.9 --n2x1 0 --c2x1 1 --p2x1 0.7 --s3 8 --vel3 1.0 --cle3 5 --clr3 3 --n3 1 --n3x0 1 --c3x0 0 --p3x0 0.9

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 6:**

New champion: 4663.32. The top 7 machines all score below 5200 and share a common structure. Let me carefully analyze the champion:

- State 0: GoToColor(clr=3/blue) → D=1(State1=Stop) on BLACK(p=0.9), → D=1(State1=Stop) on GRAY(p=0.3)
- State 1: Stop → D=2(State3!) on GRAY(p=0.85), → D=0(State0=GoToColor) on random(p=0.03), → D=2(State3!) on random(p=0.08)
- State 2: Explore(rwm=37) → D=1(State1=Stop? No...) 

Wait, with 4 states, from state 1, others are {0,2,3} indexed as D=0→State0, D=1→State2, D=2→State3.
So n1x0 D=2 → State3, n1x1 D=0 → State0, n1x2 D=2 → State3.

State2(Explore): from state 2, others are {0,1,3} indexed D=0→State0, D=1→State1, D=2→State3.
n2x0 D=1 → State1(Stop) on BLACK(p=0.9), n2x1 D=0 → State0(GoToColor) on GRAY(p=0.7).

State3(GoToColor): from state 3, others are {0,1,2} indexed D=0→State0, D=1→State1, D=2→State2.
n3x0 D=1 → State1(Stop) on BLACK(p=0.9).

So the actual flow:
- State0(GoToColor) → State1(Stop) on black/gray
- State1(Stop) → State3(GoToColor) on gray(p=0.85) or random(p=0.08), → State0(GoToColor) random(p=0.03)
- State2(Explore) → State1(Stop) on black, → State0(GoToColor) on gray — **unreachable from State1!**
- State3(GoToColor) → State1(Stop) on black

State2 is unreachable! The champion is effectively: GoToColor(0) → Stop(1) → GoToColor(3) → Stop(1), with tiny random escapes.

**The key insight confirmed:** The machine is a GoToColor ↔ Stop cycle. The Explore state (State2) is dead weight but doesn't hurt.

**What the rank 2 (4860) reveals:** It has State2=GoToColor and State3=Explore, both reachable. This is actually a different structure where Stop→GoToColor(State3), GoToColor→Stop(State1) on black/gray, and State3(Explore)→Stop on black, →GoToColor on gray. This is more like the old 3-state champion but with both GoToColor and Explore active.

**Key parameters in top machines:**
- p1x0 (Stop→next on gray): 0.85-0.9 
- p1x2 (Stop→next randomly): 0.05-0.08
- p2x1 (Explore→GoToColor on gray): 0.7-0.8
- p0x1 (GoToColor→Stop on gray): 0.3

**Strategy for Round 7:**
1. Keep champion exact
2. Tune p1x2 (0.06, 0.10, 0.12)
3. Tune p1x0 (0.88, 0.92)
4. Try making State2 reachable in the 4-state champion topology
5. Explore rank-2 topology more
6. Try p0x1 variations (0.25, 0.35)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
